{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the full scaled Bogoliubov momentum distribution for the depleted particles in the three-dimensional hard-sphere Bose gas at zero temperature. The thermodynamic limit is taken at each fixed density before the dilute limit, uniformly over all pure and mixed ground states. All thermodynamic accumulation values have the same dilute asymptotic: the limiting nonzero-momentum occupation measure has total mass $`8/(3\\sqrt\\pi)`$, giving the depletion fraction $`\\frac{8}{3\\sqrt\\pi}\\sqrt{\\rho a^3}+o(\\sqrt{\\rho a^3})`$ for density <i>ρ</i> and hard-sphere exclusion distance <i>a</i>.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the full scaled Bogoliubov momentum distribution for the depleted particles in the three-dimensional hard-sphere Bose gas at zero temperature. The thermodynamic limit is taken at each fixed density before the dilute limit, uniformly over all pure and mixed ground states. All thermodynamic accumulation values have the same dilute asymptotic: the limiting nonzero-momentum occupation measure has total mass $`8/(3\sqrt\pi)`$, giving the depletion fraction $`\frac{8}{3\sqrt\pi}\sqrt{\rho a^3}+o(\sqrt{\rho a^3})`$ for density <i>ρ</i> and hard-sphere exclusion distance <i>a</i>.

- **claim-main-momentum-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $a>0$, every bounded continuous real function $f$ on $\mathbb R^3$, and every $\varepsilon>0$, there is $\rho_0(a,f,\varepsilon)>0$ with the following property. Fix $0<\rho<\rho_0$ and put $\eta=\rho a^3$. For every sequence $N_j,L_j\longrightarrow\infty$ with $N_j/L_j^3\longrightarrow\rho$, $$\label{intro:main-limit} \limsup_{j\to\infty}\ \sup_{\Gamma\in\mathcal G_{N_j,L_j,a}} \left\| \frac{1}{N_j\sqrt\eta} \sum_{\substack{k\in(2\pi/L_j)\mathbb Z^3\\k\ne0}} f\!\left(\frac{k}{\sqrt{8\pi\rho a}}\right)n_{\Gamma,L_j}(k) -\int_{\mathbb R^3}f\,\mathrm d\nu_{\mathrm{Bog}} \right\|\le\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: In the iterated thermodynamic-then-dilute limit, the scaled momentum occupation of depleted particles converges weakly to the Bogoliubov measure, uniformly over all ground states and tested against bounded continuous functions.
- **claim-depletion-fraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-depletion)): In the same iterated limit, uniformly over all ground states, $$1-B_{N,L,a}(\Gamma) =\frac8{3\sqrt\pi}\sqrt{\rho a^3}+o(\sqrt{\rho a^3}).$$
  - Explanation: Taking the constant test function gives the depletion fraction asymptotics, uniformly over ground states.
- **claim-ground-space** (theorem, [source](#stmt-set-ground-space)): For every nonempty allowed configuration set with nontrivial form domain, the full labelled and bosonic Dirichlet operators have compact resolvent and the same lowest energy. A bosonic ground vector is therefore also a ground vector of the full labelled operator. Its modulus is a nonnegative bosonic ground vector. A form-domain function restricts, for almost every choice of some fixed coordinates, to the Dirichlet form domain of the open allowed set in the remaining coordinates. Zero extension of such a slice remains admissible if obstacles contributed by the fixed coordinates are removed.
  - Explanation: Labelled and bosonic Dirichlet operators share compact resolvent and lowest energy; the modulus of a bosonic ground vector is a nonnegative ground vector and slices stay in the Dirichlet domain.
- **claim-removal-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-set-removal)): For all sufficiently large $D$, uniformly in <a href="#set:scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="set:scales">[set:scales]</a>, $$\label{set:removal-bound} E_N-E_{N-m}\le C\alpha m\qquad(0\le m\le N).$$ At fixed $K,r$, the ratio $E_n/n$ is nondecreasing among positive particle numbers having nonempty form domain.
  - Explanation: Removing a number of particles lowers the ground energy by at most a constant times the removed number; the energy per particle is nondecreasing in particle number.
- **claim-low-count-suppression** (bound, [source](#stmt-set-low-counts)): One can fix $\alpha_0$ sufficiently large and then $\ell_*>0$ sufficiently small so that every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground function satisfies, for every $A\subset\mathcal L$, $$\label{set:low-count-bound} \mu\{n_{B_v}<\ell_*D\text{ for every }v\in A\} \le (C/D^2)^{\|A\|}.$$ The estimate holds for all sufficiently large $D$, uniformly in $K$ and the ground function.
  - Explanation: Jointly low particle counts in any set of unit cells are exponentially suppressed in the size of the set under the ground-state law.
- **claim-count-exponential-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-set-count-mgf)): For $A\subset\mathcal L$ set $$f_A(X)=\sum_{i=1}^N e^{-d_K(x_i,A)}, \qquad f_{\varnothing}=0,$$ where the distance is to the set of site centers. Every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground function obeys $$\label{set:count-mgf-bound} \log\int e^{z f_A}\,\mathrm d\mu\le CzD\|A\| \qquad(0\le z\le1).$$ The constants are uniform for $K\ge100$, $D$ sufficiently large, and $\alpha\in I_\alpha$.
  - Explanation: The exponential moment of the exponentially weighted counts grows at most linearly in the size of the cell set.
- **claim-close-neighbor-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-set-close-tail)): For each particle label $i$ let $d_i(X)=\min_{k\ne i}d_K(x_i,x_k)$, with the value $+\infty$ if $N=1$. Given $\theta>0$, one can choose $b_*>0$ sufficiently small, and then $D$ sufficiently large, so that the set $$\mathcal Q(X)=\left\{v\in\mathcal L: \#\{i:d_K(x_i,v)\le5,\ d_i(X)\le b_*D^{-1/3}\} \ge\theta D\right\}$$ satisfies, for every $A\subset\mathcal L$, $$\label{set:close-tail-bound} \mu\{A\subset\mathcal Q\}\le p_D^{\|A\|}, \qquad p_D=\bigl(C_\theta b_*^{-2}D^{-4/3}\bigr)^{1/q_*}\longrightarrow0,$$ where $q_*$ is an absolute positive integer. The uniformity is the same as in Proposition <a href="#set:count-mgf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:count-mgf">6</a>.
  - Explanation: Jointly many cells containing a positive fraction of closely spaced particles has probability decaying like a power of the cell-set size with vanishing base.
- **claim-rough-box-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-rough)): There are constants $c,C>0$ and an exponent $c_{\mathrm r}>0$, independent of the sufficiently small box exponent $\theta$, such that, for $0\le n\le6Dv_b$, $$\label{en:rough-ineq} \begin{split} H_n\ge {}&\frac{4\pi r n^2}{v_b}-Cv_bD^{1-c_{\mathrm r}} +c\,\mathrm d\Gamma\bigl(\min\{p^2,1\}\bigr)\\ &\quad+cD\sum_A \|A\|\, \mathfrak h\left(\frac{n_A}{D\|A\|}-\frac{n}{Dv_b}\right), \qquad \mathfrak h(t)=\frac{t^2}{1+\|t\|}. \end{split}$$ Here $p$ denotes the Neumann frequency and $\mathrm d\Gamma$ sums the indicated one-particle operator over all particles. One may take $c_{\mathrm r}=10^{-3}$.
  - Explanation: On a Neumann box the Hamiltonian is bounded below by the leading energy, a kinetic reserve and a penalty on count deviations.
- **claim-excitation-cutoff-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-localization)): Let $\psi$ belong to the form domain of $H_n$, and let $V_n=\sum_{i<j}v(x_i-x_j)$. Then $$\label{en:cutoff-form-error} \left\|\sum_{j=1}^3\langle F_j\psi,H_nF_j\psi\rangle -\langle\psi,H_n\psi\rangle\right\| \le\frac{C}{M^2}\left[ \langle\psi,V_n\psi\rangle +\\|v\\|_1\frac{K_H^3}{v_b}\, n\langle\psi,n_+\psi\rangle\right].$$ For any one-particle operator $a$ with $\\|a\\|\le1$, the corresponding error for $\mathrm d\Gamma(a)$ is at most $CnM^{-2}\\|\psi\\|^2$.
  - Explanation: Cutting the number of low-frequency excitations changes the form by an error controlled by the potential energy and the excitation number.
- **claim-sharp-quasiparticle-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-sharp)): For $n$ in <a href="#en:middle-sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:middle-sector">[en:middle-sector]</a> and a bosonic $n$-particle vector $\psi$ in the form domain of $H_b$ satisfying $\psi=\mathbf 1_{\{n_L\le M/4\}}\psi$, $$\label{en:sharp-bound} \langle\psi,H_b\psi\rangle \ge\{v_be_r(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\}\\|\psi\\|^2 +cE^{-2}\langle n_++K_Hn_H\rangle_\psi +c\mathcal B(\psi).$$ The constants are uniform in the particle sector and in spectator variables.
  - Explanation: On low-excitation vectors the box energy exceeds the leading scalar energy and retains both the excitation gap term and the quasiparticle energy.
- **claim-small-gap-quadratic-cubic** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-small-gap)): Let $n$ satisfy <a href="#en:middle-sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:middle-sector">[en:middle-sector]</a>, $\|z\|^2\le2n$, and $\xi=\mathbf 1_{\{n_+\le n,\ n_L\le M/4\}}\xi$. For every fixed $\delta>0$ and sufficiently large $D$, $$\begin{aligned} \langle\xi,\mathcal H_0(z)\xi\rangle &\ge 4\pi c_{\rm L}(a')^{5/2}v_b\rho_z^{5/2}\\|\xi\\|^2 +\sum_{p\ne0}\widetilde D_p(z)\\|b_p(z)\xi\\|^2 -\delta\langle S\rangle_\xi -C_\delta v_bQ^{-1/4}\\|\xi\\|^2 . \label{en:small-gap-bound} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The quadratic-cubic operator is bounded below by the scalar term and quasiparticle energy while spending only an arbitrarily small fraction of the gap.
- **claim-cosine-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-cosine-correction)): Let $n$ satisfy <a href="#en:middle-sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:middle-sector">[en:middle-sector]</a>, let $\|z\|^2\le2n$, and restrict the excitation space to $n_+\le n$ and $n_L\le M/4$. On this space the exact cosine expansion may replace the coefficients in (2.35)–(2.37) of [[FJGMOT](#ref-FJGMOT)]. Its correction is bounded in absolute quadratic form by $$\delta\left(S+\sum_{p\ne0}\widetilde D_p(z)b_p(z)^*b_p(z)\right) +C_\delta v_bQ^{-1/4}$$ for every fixed $\delta>0$, if $D$ is sufficiently large.
  - Explanation: Replacing printed cosine coefficients by exact ones costs only a small fraction of the gap and quasiparticle energy.
- **claim-four-energy-outputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-energy-outputs)): There are arbitrarily small fixed $u>0$ and then $0<w<u/10000$ for which the following statements hold. Use the nested partitions fixed in <a href="#set:nested-lengths" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="set:nested-lengths">[set:nested-lengths]</a>, with $B\asymp D^u$, $R\asymp D^w$, and $d=1/j$. For every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground vector, $$\begin{aligned} \frac1{\|\mathcal C_R\|}\sum_{A\in\mathcal C_R} \mathbb E(q_A-1)^2&\le C D^{-1-w/6},\label{en:R-count}\\ \frac1{\|\mathcal C_d\|}\sum_{A\in\mathcal C_d} \mathbb E(q_A-1)^4&\le C_jD^{-1-u/6}.\label{en:d-count} \end{aligned}$$ For every normalized, possibly complex, bosonic ground vector, $$\begin{aligned} \frac1V\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^N W_{R,i}\right\rangle &=\frac{8}{3\sqrt\pi}\alpha^{3/2}+o(1),\label{en:R-occupation}\\ \frac1V\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^N W_{d,i}\right\rangle &\le \epsilon_j+o_j(1),\qquad \epsilon_j\longrightarrow0 \quad(j\longrightarrow\infty).\label{en:d-occupation} \end{aligned}$$ Here the thermodynamic limit is taken first, at fixed dilution; the remainders then tend to zero as $D\to\infty$, and $o_j(1)$ has this meaning with $j$ fixed. The bounds are uniform over the indicated ground vectors and along every density-convergent thermodynamic sequence. A sufficiently large volume threshold may depend on the dilution and on $j$.
  - Explanation: Second and fourth moments of relative cell counts are small and the occupation of the two projections has the stated limit and vanishing behaviour.
- **claim-energy-surplus** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-surplus)): For $E\asymp Q=D^\theta$, $\theta=u$ or $w$, the following quantities have sum at most $CVQ^{-1/5}$:* 1. *the box-count penalty $$\sum_b Dv_b\,\mathfrak h\left(\frac{n_b}{Dv_b}-1\right) \quad\text{in expectation};$$* 2. *on middle-sector high pieces, the expectations of $$\sum_{p\ne0}\min(p^2,1)a_p^*a_p +D\sum_{A\in\mathcal C_d,\ A\subset b} \|A\|\mathfrak h(q_A-1);$$* 3. *on low pieces, the expectations of $E^{-2}(n_++K_Hn_H)$ and the quantities $\mathcal B(\psi)$;* 4. *on sectors outside <a href="#en:middle-sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:middle-sector">[en:middle-sector]</a>, their total $(n_b+Dv_b)$-weighted squared norms.* *The first quantity is evaluated on the original vector. The other quantities are evaluated on the indicated localized restrictions.
  - Explanation: The positive surplus terms left after comparing with the thermodynamic upper bound have small total size.
- **claim-fourth-moment-excitations** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-fourth-block)): On the $n$-particle space, for every vector $\xi$, $$\label{en:fourth-block-bound} \\|X^2\xi\\|^2\le Cn^2\langle\xi,(n_++2)^2\xi\rangle.$$
  - Explanation: The fourth-moment operator norm of a count fluctuation is bounded by the second moment of the excitation number.
- **claim-local-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mom-local-covariance)): Use the nested partitions and limiting convention of Section <a href="#set:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:section">2</a>, with the exponents chosen above. Let $A=\bigoplus_{b\in\mathcal C_B}A_b$, where the one-particle operators $A_b$ on $L^2(b)$ are identical in local coordinates and satisfy $0\le A_b\le W$. Their cosine matrices may be complex Hermitian. For every normalized bosonic ground vector, $$\label{mom:local-covariance-formula} \frac1V\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(A)\rangle =\frac1{v_b}\sum_{p\ne0}(A_b)_{pp}F_\alpha(p)+o(1), \qquad (A_b)_{pp}=\langle u_p,A_bu_p\rangle.$$ The error is uniform in the ground vector and in all the indicated operators, which may depend on the scales. The same formula and uniformity hold when $A_b=WC_bW$ and $C_b$ is any complex contraction, identical in local coordinates. Both assertions hold uniformly for simultaneous translates of the nested partitions.
  - Explanation: The expectation of a compressed one-body operator equals a trace against the Bogoliubov profile up to a uniformly small error.
- **claim-matrix-observable** (bound, [source](#stmt-en-observable)): Let $E=B$, $n$ satisfy <a href="#en:middle-sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:middle-sector">[en:middle-sector]</a>, and $\|z\|^2\le2n$. For $0\le A_b\le W$ and an excitation vector $\xi$ in the form domain of $n_+$, put $$\mathcal Q_z(\xi)=\sum_{p\ne0}\widetilde D_p(z)\\|b_p(z)\xi\\|^2, \qquad T_A(z)=\sum_{p\ne0}(A_b)_{pp}\|V_p(z)\|^2,$$ where $a_p=U_pb_p+V_pb_p^*$ is the inverse of <a href="#en:bogoliubov" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:bogoliubov">[en:bogoliubov]</a>, with the phase of $z$ restored. Then $0\le T_A(z)\le Cv_b$ and $$\begin{aligned} \left\|\langle\xi,\mathrm d\Gamma(A_b)\xi\rangle -T_A(z)\\|\xi\\|^2\right\| &\le C(1+R^2)\mathcal Q_z(\xi)\nonumber\\ &\quad+C\{(1+R^2)\mathcal Q_z(\xi) T_A(z)\\|\xi\\|^2\}^{1/2}. \label{en:observable-bound} \end{aligned}$$ The constant is independent of $A_b$.
  - Explanation: A matrix observable is compared with its Bogoliubov trace with error controlled by the quasiparticle energy.
- **claim-good-site-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-good-probability)): The constants in Definition <a href="#lp:certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lp:certificate">18</a> can be chosen so that $$\label{lp:good-probability-bounds} \mathbb P\{v\text{ is bad}\}\leq C(x)D^{-1-1/8}, \qquad \mathbb P\{A\text{ consists of bad sites}\}\leq p^{\|A\|}$$ for every site $v$ and every set $A$ of distinct sites. Here $p>0$ can be any prescribed fixed constant, followed by a sufficiently large lower threshold on $D$. Failure of the full-path event in a fixed neighborhood has probability at most $C(x)D^{-6}$. The estimates are uniform in $\Phi$ and in all sufficiently large torus sizes $K$.
  - Explanation: Bad sites are rare, jointly with geometric decay in the size of a set of sites.
- **claim-collision-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-collision)): Fix the exposed data in a good neighborhood. Let $k$ be a deterministic path satisfying its individual cutoffs, and let $i\ne k$ have the eligible endpoints. Under $B_i$, the probability that the trial satisfies its own cutoffs and comes within distance $r$ of path $k$ is at most $\beta_{ik}$. For two legs pinned at a prescribed midpoint $y$, with $\|y-m_i\|\leq8b$ and $d_K(y,\omega_k(0))>s$, the bound has the additional term $$\frac{b_D}{s\vee d_K(y,\omega_k(0))}.$$ If $y$ is instead uniform in a unit cell at a fixed bounded distance from $m_i$, the integrated collision probability, including failure of this midpoint separation, is at most $C\beta_{ik}$.
  - Explanation: A trial bridge collides with a fixed allowed path with small probability.
- **claim-insertion-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-insertion)): Fix good data in all neighborhoods met by the following trials. In the first three assertions existing unexposed paths may take any values satisfying their individual cutoffs, while the frozen paths remain those of the fixed data. The fourth assertion instead concerns an obstacle collection satisfying the full-path bounds. For all sufficiently large $D$, these assertions hold also when an arbitrary subset of avoidance checks is omitted.* 1. *An ordinary trial $B_i$, required to satisfy its own cutoffs and avoid the frozen paths and all indicated other unexposed paths, succeeds with probability at least $3/4$.* 2. *A trial formed by choosing a uniform midpoint in a unit cell contained in $B(m_i,8b)$ and at an absolute constant distance from its mean, then choosing independent bridge legs, has success probability at least $3/4$. It may also be required to have midpoint distance greater than $s$ from every opponent.* 3. *Two such trials in two copies, using a common unit cell meeting the preceding conditions for both labels, with a common uniform midpoint and independent legs conditional on that midpoint, simultaneously succeed with probability at least $1/2$. Each is checked against the previously present paths in its own copy; the pair is not checked against itself across the copies.* 4. *Suppose the entire obstacle collection satisfies the motion and count bounds defining the full-path event. A trial pinned at any point within $8b$ of its mean and more than $s$ from every obstacle midpoint succeeds with probability at least $3/4$. This remains valid after adding or substituting a fixed number of individually allowed obstacles, provided the indicated endpoint and midpoint separations hold.
  - Explanation: Trial bridges can be inserted avoiding obstacles with probability bounded away from zero.
- **claim-trial-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-trial-density)): Fix good data and a set of route labels $S\subset\mathcal I$ with a bounded number of labels per site. In a product of their free bridge probabilities, condition on individual cutoffs and any selection of avoidance checks, including checks against fixed outside paths. The paths with labels in $\mathcal I\setminus S$ need only satisfy their individual cutoffs; the frozen paths remain those of the fixed good data. Every specified $q$ labels have joint marginal density at most $C^q$ relative to the product of their $B_i$. The conclusion also holds conditional on the values of all other labels. A uniform-midpoint insertion has the additional density expense $CD^{Cx}$ for each inserted marginal, or in an augmented integral retaining the common midpoint, and replacing one free midpoint density by that of a neighboring label has expense <a href="#lp:midpoint-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lp:midpoint-ratio">[lp:midpoint-ratio]</a>.
  - Explanation: Conditioning on avoidance changes the joint marginal density of selected labels by a bounded factor per label.
- **claim-local-deletion-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-palm-local-comparison)): With the exponents and partitions of Proposition <a href="#en:energy-outputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="en:energy-outputs">13</a>, every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground function satisfies $$\label{palm:local-rms-comparison} \frac1{\|\mathcal C_d\|}\sum_{v\sim w} \int\frac{\|s_v-s_w\|^4}{s_v^2+s_w^2}\,\mathrm dY' \le C_jD^{-1-u/8}.$$ Here $v\sim w$ ranges over ordered adjacent $d$-cells, and the quotient is zero when both functions vanish. The sum may be restricted to pairs in the same $R$-cell. The bound is uniform in the ground function and volume, with the limiting convention of Section <a href="#set:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:section">2</a>.
  - Explanation: Smoothed deletion densities of adjacent small cells are close in a weighted fourth-moment sense.
- **claim-augmented-reference** (theorem, [source](#stmt-palm-reference)): The projection of $P_{\mathrm{aug},v}$ just described is $P_v$. The mass of $P_v$ equals $\mathbb ET_v^{\mathrm{rel}}$. Its average over either partition is one.
  - Explanation: The projection of the augmented reference measure is the smoothed deletion measure, whose mass is the expected relative count and averages to one.
- **claim-flagged-count-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-palm-counts)): The count estimates of Proposition <a href="#en:energy-outputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="en:energy-outputs">13</a> imply $$\begin{aligned} \frac1{\|\mathcal C_R\|}\sum_{v\in\mathcal C_R} \mathbb E\|T_v^{\mathrm{rel}}-1\|^2 &\le C\bigl(D^{-1-w/6}+D^{-1+x}R^{-3}\bigr), \label{palm:R-count-moment}\\ \frac1{\|\mathcal C_d\|}\sum_{v\in\mathcal C_d} \mathbb E\|T_v^{\mathrm{rel}}-1\|^4 &\le C_j\bigl(D^{-1-u/6}+D^{-2+2x}\bigr). \label{palm:d-count-moment} \end{aligned}$$ For each fixed integer $q$, products of at most $q$ relative counts can be bounded by $C_jD^{qx}$ at an error smaller than every prescribed negative power of $D$. This assertion holds in any of the averaged estimates involving a fixed number of cells used below.
  - Explanation: Fluctuations of the relative number of flagged particles are controlled by the count estimates.
- **claim-omission-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-palm-omissions)): For every indicated cell $v$, the mass in $P_{\mathrm{aug},v}$ of tags outside $\mathcal I$ is at most $C_{x,j}D^{-1-1/8}$, uniformly in $v$. The mass lost by a failure of the required good neighborhood is at most $C_{x,j}D^{-1-1/8+x}$ for a $d$-cell and at most $C_xR^3D^{-1-1/8+x}$ for an $R$-cell. Multiplying an omission by a fixed product of relative counts costs at most a further factor $C_jD^{qx}$, with $q$ the number of factors. The same convention applies to a fixed power of the omitted relative count itself.
  - Explanation: Mass discarded by omitting tags or bad neighborhoods is smaller than the inverse density by a power.
- **claim-two-copy-transport** (construction, [source](#stmt-palm-transport)): For adjacent $d$-cells on the retained data event one can choose <a href="#palm:label-list" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="palm:label-list">[palm:label-list]</a> of length $m\le C_x$, with $C_x=O(x^{-1/2})$, and construct two path copies with these properties:* 1. *All unselected paths are the same as in the original sample. The left path of $i$ and the right path of $j$ are also unchanged.* 2. *Both copies satisfy every path constraint defining the conditional law at the given datum.* 3. *For $1\le k\le m$, the left midpoint of $i_k$ equals the right midpoint of $i_{k-1}$. Thus, after deleting $i$ on the left and $j$ on the right, the midpoint configurations agree as unordered lists.* 4. *The construction is a probability kernel conditional on the original sample and the route choices.* *Consequently, weighting by <a href="#palm:two-tag-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="palm:two-tag-weight">[palm:two-tag-weight]</a>, averaging all choices, and ordering the common remaining midpoint list uniformly defines a single finite measure $\nu_{v,w}$ on the remaining particles.
  - Explanation: Two path copies are built so that after deleting one particle in each, the remaining midpoint configurations agree.
- **claim-likelihood-centering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-palm-likelihood)): The left-output likelihood, with the weight $h_{w,j}$ included but the divisor $T_w$ omitted, is, on admissible reference values, $$\label{palm:likelihood-formula} L_S(z;\xi) =\int a_S(o;\xi)h_{w,j}(o) k_S(o;\xi,z)\prod_{l\in S}B_l(\mathrm do_l).$$ It satisfies $$\label{palm:centered-identity} F_S=L_S-h_{w,j},\qquad \mathbb E_\lambda[F_S\mid\omega_{S^c}]=0.$$ These assertions hold after removing any specified collection of outside checks, provided the same removals are used in the reference, the original-path integral, and the insertion kernels. Moreover, $$\label{palm:local-likelihood-sup} 0\le L_S\le C_xD^{C\sqrt x}.$$
  - Explanation: The conditional normalizer cancels, the centered likelihood has zero conditional mean and is bounded.
- **claim-two-switch-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-palm-two-switches)): Write $\beta_{lk}$ for the collision bound in Lemma <a href="#lp:collision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lp:collision">20</a>, and set $$\beta_* = C_xD^{-19/100}.$$ Suppose either that the four changed sets are disjoint, or that they have exactly one independent label identification. After increasing $D$ the following bounds hold for their centered likelihoods, integrated against the conditional reference on their union. Finite differences act on the entire admissibility numerator and its likelihood factors; the original conditional-reference normalizer is held fixed.* 1. *A finite difference that switches one block-pair interaction has absolute integral at most $C_xD^{C\sqrt x}$ times the sum of the $\beta_{lk}$ for the finitely many label pairs checked across that interaction. In particular it is at most $C_xD^{C\sqrt x}\beta_*$.* 2. *If all four lists have disjoint changed sets, a mixed difference in two distinct block-pair interactions has absolute integral at most $C_xD^{C\sqrt x}$ times a finite sum of products $\beta_{lk}\beta_{l'k'}$, with one checked pair from each interaction.* *The bounds are valid if additional switches have already been fixed on or off. The number of summands is bounded by a constant depending only on $x$.
  - Explanation: Switching cross-block checks changes centered likelihood integrals by collision-sized amounts, with one or two forced collisions.
- **claim-centered-fourth-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-palm-fourth)): For adjacent $d$-cells $v,w$, let an overbar denote the average over the route and interior label choices at fixed tags and datum. Restrict the expectation to $\mathcal G_{v,w}$ and retained first tags. Equivalently, set the complete centered summand to zero outside this event. Then $$\label{palm:fourth-centered} \mathbb E\left[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G_{v,w}} \sum_{i\in\mathcal I}\frac{h_{v,i}}{T_v} \left\|\sum_{j\ne i}\frac{\overline{F_S}}{T_w}\right\|^4\right] \le C_{x,j}D^{-1-1/20},$$ provided $x$ is sufficiently small. The candidate second tags are the labels in $\mathcal I$ whose mean-sites lie in the required enlargement of $w$.
  - Explanation: The fourth moment of the averaged centered likelihood has a power saving.
- **claim-augmented-fourth-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-palm-augmented-comparison)): For sufficiently small $x>0$, depending on the previously chosen exponent $u$, the measures above satisfy $$\label{palm:augmented-fourth} \frac1{\|\mathcal C_d\|}\sum_{v\sim w} \left\{\int\|\ell_v-1\|^4\,\mathrm dP_{\mathrm{aug},v} +\int\|\ell_w-1\|^4\,\mathrm dP_{\mathrm{aug},w}\right\} \le C_jD^{-1-u/8}.$$ The summation can be restricted to adjacent cells in the same $R$-cell. The bound is uniform in volume and in the nonnegative ground function, with the limiting convention of Section <a href="#set:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:section">2</a>.
  - Explanation: Augmented likelihood densities deviate from one by a small fourth moment, averaged over adjacent cells.
- **claim-random-routes** (construction, [source](#stmt-rt-routes)): There are $p_0,\kappa,c,C>0$ and $R_0$ with the following property. Suppose $R\ge R_0$, $K\ge C_0R$, and <a href="#rt:bad-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rt:bad-law">[rt:bad-law]</a> holds with $p\le p_0$. Put $h=\lfloor R^{1/2}\rfloor$. There is an event $\mathcal E$, determined by $\mathcal D,z,Q$, on which the construction below gives simple good nearest-neighbor routes $\pi_y$ from $z$ to every $y\in Q$. Conditional on the datum, routes are sampled using fresh independent random variables. The event includes the requirement that the $10R$-neighborhoods of $z$ and $Q$ contain no bad sites.* *For a route $\pi=(v_0,\ldots,v_m)$ starting at $z$, set $$a(\pi)=\max\{i:d_\infty(v_i,z)\le h\},\qquad \pi^{\rm ch}=\{v_i:i>a(\pi)\}.$$ Its silent portion $(v_0,\ldots,v_{a(\pi)})$ has at most $Ch$ vertices. The analogous silent portion read backwards from $y$ also has at most $Ch$ vertices, and the two portions are disjoint. Let $\pi^{\rm ch,q}$ consist of vertices whose path index is within $q$ of an index in $\pi^{\rm ch}$. For independently sampled routes $\pi_y,\pi_{y'}$, define $$\begin{aligned} J&=\sum_{v\in\pi_y}\sum_{v'\in\pi_{y'}} \mathbf1_{\{d_\infty(v,v')\le r_c\}},\\ J_*&=\sum_{v\in\pi_y^{\rm ch,q}} \sum_{v'\in\pi_{y'}^{\rm ch,q}} \mathbf1_{\{d_\infty(v,v')\le r_c\}}. \end{aligned}$$ For every fixed pair $y,y'\in Q$, $$\label{rt:exponential} \mathbb E_{\mathcal D}\!\left[ \mathbf1_{\mathcal E}\, \mathbb E_{\rm routes}(e^{\kappa J}\mid\mathcal D)\right]\le C.$$ For every deterministic probability mass function $\omega$ on $Q\times Q$ satisfying $\omega(y,y')\le C_\omega R^{-6}$, $$\label{rt:rare} \sum_{y,y'\in Q}\omega(y,y')\, \mathbb E_{\mathcal D}\!\left[ \mathbf1_{\mathcal E}\, \mathbb P_{\rm routes}(J_*>0\mid\mathcal D)\right] \le CC_\omega R^{-c_g},\qquad c_g=\frac12\left(\frac{\log2}{\log3}-\frac12\right)>0.$$ The same conclusions hold with a common terminal endpoint, reading the routes in reverse order.* *If $\mathcal M$ denotes just the stated bad-free margin event, then $$\label{rt:rejection} \mathbb P(\mathcal M\setminus\mathcal E) \le K^3(Ap)^{\lfloor K/(10(r_c+2))\rfloor} +CR^3e^{-cR}.$$ Here $A$ is fixed and $p_0$ is chosen so that $Ap_0<1$.
  - Explanation: Random lattice routes avoiding bad components have exponentially integrable total encounters and rare encounters after the silent portions.
- **claim-unpredictable-walk** (construction, [source](#stmt-rt-prediction)): Put $\sigma=\log2/\log3$. For every integer $T\ge1$ there is an integer-valued process $(S_j)_{j=0}^T$, with $S_0=0$ and $\|S_{j+1}-S_j\|=1$, such that $$\label{rt:prediction-bound} \sup_{x\in\mathbb Z} \mathbb P(S_{u+k}=x\mid S_0,\ldots,S_u) \le 6^\sigma k^{-\sigma} \quad(k\ge1,\ u+k\le T).$$
  - Explanation: A nearest-neighbour integer process has conditional atoms decaying as a power of the lag.
- **claim-ordered-targets** (bound, [source](#stmt-rt-ordered)): At each time $s\in\{0,\ldots,T\}$, let $A_s(l)$ be a union of at most two deterministic squares of maximum radius $l$, with arbitrary centers, in the transverse torus. There is a constant $C_1$, independent of their centers and of $T\le K/2$, such that $$\label{rt:ordered-bound} \sum_{0\le s_1<\cdots<s_j\le T} \mathbb P\{X_{s_i}\in A_{s_i}(l_i),\ 1\le i\le j\} \le \prod_{i=1}^jC_1(1+l_i)^2.$$ In particular, for $a_l\ge0$, if $C_1\sum_{l\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2<1$, then $$\label{rt:ordered-product} \mathbb E\prod_{s=0}^T \left(1+\sum_{l\ge0}a_l\mathbf1_{\{X_s\in A_s(l)\}}\right) \le \left(1-C_1\sum_{l\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2\right)^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Ordered hitting probabilities of moving targets factor into geometric products.
- **claim-distant-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-long-comparison)): For sufficiently small $x>0$ relative to the previously fixed exponents, the functions $s_v$ of Lemma <a href="#palm:reference" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="palm:reference">24</a> satisfy $$\label{long:hellinger} \frac1{\|\mathcal A_{K,R}\|}\sum_{(v,w)\in\mathcal A_{K,R}} \int \|s_v(Y')-s_w(Y')\|^2\,\mathrm dY' =o(D^{-1})+o_K(1).$$ The $o(D^{-1})$ is uniform over the nonnegative ground vectors and over the fixed window of $\alpha$. The thermodynamic error is taken to zero first.
  - Explanation: Averaged over distant cell pairs, the squared distance between root deletion densities is below the inverse density scale.
- **claim-transport-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-mass)): For some $c_3>0$, the mass of this common measure satisfies, after averaging over $(v,w)\in\mathcal A_{K,R}$, $$\label{long:mass-bound} \nu_{vw}(1)\ge \mathbb E[T_v^{\rm rel}T_w^{\rm rel}]-C D^{-1-c_3}-o_K(1).$$
  - Explanation: The common transport measure retains almost all of the product of relative counts.
- **claim-reference-normalizers** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-normalizers)): If $S\cap T=\varnothing$, then $$\label{long:disjoint-normalizers} 1\le\frac{Z_S^-Z_T^-}{Z_U} \le \exp(CB_{ST}).$$ If $q=\|S\cap T\|$ is arbitrary, then $$\label{long:shared-normalizers} \frac{Z_S^-Z_T^-}{Z_U} \le C^q\exp(CB_{ST}).$$ All normalizers are strictly positive. In the disjoint case, if $H$ is any measurable function of the latent originals with $0\le H\le1$, and $A^- = A_S^- A_T^-$, then $$\label{long:tag-integral-difference} 0\le \frac1{Z_U}\int H(A^- - A_U)\,\mathrm dB_U \le C e^{CB_{ST}} B_{ST}.$$
  - Explanation: Removing cross checks changes reference normalizers by a controlled factor.
- **claim-transition-factors** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-transitions)): Use the same latent originals and the same generated variables in the original and separate protocols. The original integrand is bounded by the separate integrand times a nonnegative factor $\mathcal R$ satisfying $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal R&\le e^{C D^{-19/100}J}, \label{long:factor-exponential}\\ \mathcal R&\le 1+C e^{C D^{-19/100}J}(B_{ST}+M_{ST}). \label{long:factor-increment} \end{aligned}$$ The assertion is for the product of the two protocols as well. A transition with no removed check has ratio exactly one.
  - Explanation: Original and separate protocol integrands differ by a factor controlled by the contact count.
- **claim-sequential-kernels** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-kernel)): Let $\mu(\mathrm dx)$ be a probability measure of latent variables, followed by a finite ordered list of subprobability kernels. At stage $r$ the kernel generates a primary variable $z_r$ and arbitrary auxiliary variables. Let $Q$ be a set of stages. Suppose that, for every latent input and every allowed preceding history, the primary marginal at $r\in Q$ is at most $A_r\nu_r(x,\mathrm dz_r)$, where $\nu_r$ is a subprobability kernel depending on $x$ but not on the preceding history. Then the joint measure of the selected primary outputs is at most $$\label{long:reverse-kernel} \left(\prod_{r\in Q}A_r\right) \int\mu(\mathrm dx)\prod_{r\in Q}\nu_r(x,\mathrm dz_r).$$
  - Explanation: The joint law of selected primary outputs of sequential subprobability kernels is dominated by the product of marginal bounds.
- **claim-shared-marginal-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-shared-density)): Divide the separate output measure $M_S^-$ by its reference normalizer $Z_S^-$, and omit its original tag indicator if desired. For any $q$ specified primary output labels, its marginal is dominated by $$\label{long:marginal-bound} G_D^q\bigotimes_{l\text{ specified}} B_l, \qquad G_D=C D^{C\sqrt x}.$$ The same assertion holds for $T$, and the constants are independent of the number of unselected outputs.
  - Explanation: Marginals of specified outputs are dominated by bridge laws times a mild per-label factor.
- **claim-likelihood-product** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-product)): At almost every fixed admissible outside of $U=S\cup T$, the likelihoods of the two original transports satisfy the following bounds. If $S,T$ are disjoint, then $$\label{long:disjoint-product} \mathbb E[L_SL_T\mid U^c]\le \mathbb E[h_{w,j}h_{w,j'}\mid U^c] +C e^{C D^{-19/100}J} \bigl(B_{ST}+J_*b_D(1+t^{-3/2})\bigr).$$ If $q=\|S\cap T\|\ge1$, then $$\label{long:shared-product} \mathbb E[L_SL_T\mid U^c] \le e^{C D^{-19/100}J}G_D^q,$$ after increasing the fixed constant in $G_D$.
  - Explanation: The product of two transport likelihoods is bounded by the tag indicators plus cost terms, or by a per-shared-label factor.
- **claim-label-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-label-average)): At fixed retained data and route sites, the label average of the error in <a href="#long:disjoint-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="long:disjoint-product">[long:disjoint-product]</a>, together with the contribution of all choices having $S\cap T\ne\varnothing$, is at most $$\label{long:label-error} C D^{-1+\zeta(x)}J_*e^{\delta_D J}, \qquad \delta_D\longrightarrow0, \qquad \zeta(x)\longrightarrow0\quad(x\downarrow0).$$ This estimate includes the normalized sums over the three tag labels at their specified mean-sites. The remaining weight of each tag site is at most $CR^{-3}$.
  - Explanation: Averaging over labels suppresses likelihood errors and shared-label collisions to a power saving.
- **claim-augmented-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-long-augmented-moments)): For some $c_4>0$, the left augmented likelihood satisfies $$\label{long:second-moment} \int\ell_{v,w}^2\,\mathrm dP_{{\rm aug},v} \le \mathbb E[T_v^{\rm rel}(T_w^{\rm rel})^2] +CD^{-1-c_4}+o_K(1)$$ after averaging over $\mathcal A_{K,R}$. Its mass satisfies <a href="#long:mass-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="long:mass-bound">[long:mass-bound]</a>, and the same estimates hold with $v,w$ exchanged.
  - Explanation: The second moment of the augmented likelihood is bounded by the expected relative-count term plus a power saving.
- **claim-far-pairs-variance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-asm-far-pairs)): Let $\mathcal H$ be a complex Hilbert space and let $f_z\in\mathcal H$, $z\in(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^3$. Set $$I_M=\{h\in\{0,\ldots,M-1\}:.30M\le h\le .45M\}, \qquad \overline f=M^{-3}\sum_z f_z.$$ For all sufficiently large $M$, there is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\label{asm:far-variance} \frac1{M^3}\sum_z\\|f_z-\overline f\\|_{\mathcal H}^2 \le \frac{C}{M^3\|I_M\|M^2} \sum_z\sum_{h_1\in I_M}\sum_{h_2,h_3\in\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z} \\|f_{z+h}-f_z\\|_{\mathcal H}^2.$$
  - Explanation: Variance of a Hilbert-valued array on the torus is controlled by squared differences over distant pairs.
- **claim-rms-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-asm-rms-interpolation)): For any finite nonempty array of nonnegative numbers $y_i$, with uniform averaging denoted by $\mathbb E_i$, put $a=\mathbb E_i y_i^2$ and $m=\mathbb E_i y_i$. Then $$\label{asm:rms-inequality} (\sqrt a-m)^2\le\frac12\mathbb E_{i,i'} \frac{(y_i-y_{i'})^4}{y_i^2+y_{i'}^2}.$$ The quotient is defined to be zero when its denominator is zero. Furthermore, for every $y,z\ge0$, $$\label{asm:sqrt-comparison} \|\sqrt y-\sqrt z\|^4 \le\frac{(y-z)^4}{y^2+z^2} \le8\|\sqrt y-\sqrt z\|^4.$$
  - Explanation: The squared gap between root mean square and mean is bounded by a fourth-moment quotient.
- **claim-infrared-excitations** (bound, [source](#stmt-asm-infrared)): Put $Q_R=P_R-P_0$. Uniformly over normalized, possibly complex, bosonic ground vectors, $$\label{asm:infrared-bound} D\\|(Q_R)_1\Phi\\|_2^2\le16\epsilon_j+o(1)+o_K(1),$$ where $\epsilon_j\to0$. The coefficient $16$ is independent of $j$; the dilute remainder may depend on $j$. The exponents $u,w$ and the scale $R$ may be kept fixed when $j$ is increased; all dilute and volume thresholds may depend on $j$.
  - Explanation: Excitations between cell-constant functions and the condensate orbital have vanishing density, uniformly over ground vectors.
- **claim-shift-averaging** (bound, [source](#stmt-mom-shift-average)): If $K>2(B+H)$ and $\|h\|\le H$, then $$\label{mom:shift-norm} \left\\|T_h-\operatorname{Avg}_{\mathcal C\in\mathcal S} T_h^{\mathcal C}\right\\| \le C\frac{H+R}{B}.$$
  - Explanation: Averaging cut translations over shifted box partitions approximates translation in operator norm.
- **claim-characteristic-integrals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mom-characteristic)): For every fixed $H<\infty$, $$\label{mom:characteristic-limit} \int e^{ih\cdot p}\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m_\Phi(p) =\int_{\mathbb R^3}e^{ih\cdot p}F_\alpha(p) \frac{\mathrm dp}{(2\pi)^3}+o(1), \qquad \|h\|\le H.$$ The error is uniform in $h$ and in normalized, possibly complex, ground vectors, with the iterated-limit convention of Section <a href="#set:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:section">2</a>.
  - Explanation: Characteristic integrals of the momentum measure converge to the Fourier transform of the Bogoliubov profile uniformly on bounded sets.

          "exact_quote": "For $A\\subset\\mathcal L$ set $$f_A(X)=\\sum_{i=1}^N e^{-d_K(x_i,A)},\n \\qquad f_{\\varnothing}=0,$$ where the distance is to the set of site centers. Every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground function obeys $$\\label{set:count-mgf-bound}\n \\log\\int e^{z f_A}\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\\le CzD|A|\n               \\qquad(0\\le z\\le1).$$ The constants are uniform for $K\\ge100$, $D$ sufficiently large, and $\\alpha\\in I_\\alpha$.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-set-close-tail",
        "exact_quote": "For each particle label $i$ let $d_i(X)=\\min_{k\\ne i}d_K(x_i,x_k)$, with the value $+\\infty$ if $N=1$. Given $\\theta>0$, one can choose $b_*>0$ sufficiently small, and then $D$ sufficiently large, so that the set $$\\mathcal Q(X)=\\left\\{v\\in\\mathcal L:\n    \\#\\{i:d_K(x_i,v)\\le5,\\ d_i(X)\\le b_*D^{-1/3}\\}\n          \\ge\\theta D\\right\\}$$ satisfies, for every $A\\subset\\mathcal L$, $$\\label{set:close-tail-bound}\n \\mu\\{A\\subset\\mathcal Q\\}\\le p_D^{|A|},\n \\qquad\n p_D=\\bigl(C_\\theta b_*^{-2}D^{-4/3}\\bigr)^{1/q_*}\\longrightarrow0,$$ where $q_*$ is an absolute positive integer. The uniformity is the same as in Proposition <a href=\"#set:count-mgf\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"set:count-mgf\">6</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-en-rough",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"en:rough-bound\" label=\"en:rough-bound\"></span>There are constants $c,C>0$ and an exponent $c_{\\mathrm r}>0$, independent of the sufficiently small box exponent $\\theta$, such that, for $0\\le n\\le6Dv_b$, $$\\label{en:rough-ineq}\n \\begin{split}\n H_n\\ge {}&\\frac{4\\pi r n^2}{v_b}-Cv_bD^{1-c_{\\mathrm r}}\n       +c\\,\\mathrm d\\Gamma\\bigl(\\min\\{p^2,1\\}\\bigr)\\\\\n &\\quad+cD\\sum_A |A|\\,\n   \\mathfrak h\\left(\\frac{n_A}{D|A|}-\\frac{n}{Dv_b}\\right),\n \\qquad \\mathfrak h(t)=\\frac{t^2}{1+|t|}.\n \\end{split}$$ Here $p$ denotes the Neumann frequency and $\\mathrm d\\Gamma$ sums the indicated one-particle operator over all particles.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-en-localization",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"en:cutoff-error\" label=\"en:cutoff-error\"></span>Let $\\psi$ belong to the form domain of $H_n$, and let $V_n=\\sum_{i<j}v(x_i-x_j)$. Then $$\\label{en:cutoff-form-error}\n \\left|\\sum_{j=1}^3\\langle F_j\\psi,H_nF_j\\psi\\rangle\n                   -\\langle\\psi,H_n\\psi\\rangle\\right|\n \\le\\frac{C}{M^2}\\left[\n     \\langle\\psi,V_n\\psi\\rangle\n       +\\|v\\|_1\\frac{K_H^3}{v_b}\\,\n                  n\\langle\\psi,n_+\\psi\\rangle\\right].$$ For any one-particle operator $a$ with $\\|a\\|\\le1$, the corresponding error for $\\mathrm d\\Gamma(a)$ is at most $CnM^{-2}\\|\\psi\\|^2$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-en-sharp",
        "exact_quote": "For $n$ in <a href=\"#en:middle-sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"en:middle-sector\">[en:middle-sector]</a> and a bosonic $n$-particle vector $\\psi$ in the form domain of $H_b$ satisfying $\\psi=\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_L\\le M/4\\}}\\psi$, $$\\label{en:sharp-bound}\n \\langle\\psi,H_b\\psi\\rangle\n \\ge\\{v_be_r(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\\}\\|\\psi\\|^2\n       +cE^{-2}\\langle n_++K_Hn_H\\rangle_\\psi\n       +c\\mathcal B(\\psi).$$ The constants are uniform in the particle sector and in spectator variables.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $n$ in <a href=\"#en:middle-sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"en:middle-sector\">[en:middle-sector]</a> and a bosonic $n$-particle vector $\\psi$ in the form domain of $H_b$ satisfying $\\psi=\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_L\\le M/4\\}}\\psi$, $$\\label{en:sharp-bound}\n \\langle\\psi,H_b\\psi\\rangle\n \\ge\\{v_be_r(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\\}\\|\\psi\\|^2\n       +cE^{-2}\\langle n_++K_Hn_H\\rangle_\\psi\n       +c\\mathcal B(\\psi).$$ The constants are uniform in the particle sector and in spectator variables.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $n$ satisfy <a href=\"#en:middle-sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"en:middle-sector\">[en:middle-sector]</a>, $|z|^2\\le2n$, and $\\xi=\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_+\\le n,\\ n_L\\le M/4\\}}\\xi$. For every fixed $\\delta>0$ and sufficiently large $D$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\langle\\xi,\\mathcal H_0(z)\\xi\\rangle\n &\\ge 4\\pi c_{\\rm L}(a')^{5/2}v_b\\rho_z^{5/2}\\|\\xi\\|^2\n       +\\sum_{p\\ne0}\\widetilde D_p(z)\\|b_p(z)\\xi\\|^2\n       -\\delta\\langle S\\rangle_\\xi\n       -C_\\delta v_bQ^{-1/4}\\|\\xi\\|^2 .\n                                                        \\label{en:small-gap-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-en-cosine-correction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n$ satisfy <a href=\"#en:middle-sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"en:middle-sector\">[en:middle-sector]</a>, let $|z|^2\\le2n$, and restrict the excitation space to $n_+\\le n$ and $n_L\\le M/4$. On this space the exact cosine expansion may replace the coefficients in (2.35)–(2.37) of [[FJGMOT](#ref-FJGMOT)]. Its correction is bounded in absolute quadratic form by $$\\delta\\left(S+\\sum_{p\\ne0}\\widetilde D_p(z)b_p(z)^*b_p(z)\\right)\n       +C_\\delta v_bQ^{-1/4}$$ for every fixed $\\delta>0$, if $D$ is sufficiently large.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },

On this space the exact cosine expansion may replace the coefficients in (2.35)–(2.37) of [[FJGMOT](#ref-FJGMOT)].     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-en-energy-outputs",
        "exact_quote": "There are arbitrarily small fixed $u>0$ and then $0<w<u/10000$ for which the following statements hold. Use the nested partitions fixed in <a href=\"#set:nested-lengths\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"set:nested-lengths\">[set:nested-lengths]</a>, with $B\\asymp D^u$, $R\\asymp D^w$, and $d=1/j$. For every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground vector, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1{|\\mathcal C_R|}\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_R}\n       \\mathbb E(q_A-1)^2&\\le C D^{-1-w/6},\\label{en:R-count}\\\\\n \\frac1{|\\mathcal C_d|}\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_d}\n       \\mathbb E(q_A-1)^4&\\le C_jD^{-1-u/6}.\\label{en:d-count}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every normalized, possibly complex, bosonic ground vector, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1V\\left\\langle\\sum_{i=1}^N W_{R,i}\\right\\rangle\n       &=\\frac{8}{3\\sqrt\\pi}\\alpha^{3/2}+o(1),\\label{en:R-occupation}\\\\\n \\frac1V\\left\\langle\\sum_{i=1}^N W_{d,i}\\right\\rangle\n       &\\le \\epsilon_j+o_j(1),\\qquad \\epsilon_j\\longrightarrow0\n                \\quad(j\\longrightarrow\\infty).\\label{en:d-occupation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here the thermodynamic limit is taken first, at fixed dilution; the remainders then tend to zero as $D\\to\\infty$, and $o_j(1)$ has this meaning with $j$ fixed. The bounds are uniform over the indicated ground vectors and along every density-convergent thermodynamic sequence. A sufficiently large volume threshold may depend on the dilution and on $j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-en-energy-outputs",
          "exact_quote": "There are arbitrarily small fixed $u>0$ and then $0<w<u/10000$ for which the following statements hold. Use the nested partitions fixed in <a href=\"#set:nested-lengths\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"set:nested-lengths\">[set:nested-lengths]</a>, with $B\\asymp D^u$, $R\\asymp D^w$, and $d=1/j$. For every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground vector, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1{|\\mathcal C_R|}\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_R}\n       \\mathbb E(q_A-1)^2&\\le C D^{-1-w/6},\\label{en:R-count}\\\\\n \\frac1{|\\mathcal C_d|}\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_d}\n       \\mathbb E(q_A-1)^4&\\le C_jD^{-1-u/6}.\\label{en:d-count}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every normalized, possibly complex, bosonic ground vector, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac1V\\left\\langle\\sum_{i=1}^N W_{R,i}\\right\\rangle\n       &=\\frac{8}{3\\sqrt\\pi}\\alpha^{3/2}+o(1),\\label{en:R-occupation}\\\\\n \\frac1V\\left\\langle\\sum_{i=1}^N W_{d,i}\\right\\rangle\n       &\\le \\epsilon_j+o_j(1),\\qquad \\epsilon_j\\longrightarrow0\n                \\quad(j\\longrightarrow\\infty).\\label{en:d-occupation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here the thermodynamic limit is taken first, at fixed dilution; the remainders then tend to zero as $D\\to\\infty$, and $o_j(1)$ has this meaning with $j$ fixed. The bounds are uniform over the indicated ground vectors and along every density-convergent thermodynamic sequence. A sufficiently large volume threshold may depend on the dilution and on $j$.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are arbitrarily small fixed $u>0$ and then $0<w<u/10000$ for which the following statements hold.         "anchor": "stmt-mom-local-covariance",
        "exact_quote": "Use the nested partitions and limiting convention of Section <a href=\"#set:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"set:section\">2</a>, with the exponents chosen above. Let $A=\\bigoplus_{b\\in\\mathcal C_B}A_b$, where the one-particle operators $A_b$ on $L^2(b)$ are identical in local coordinates and satisfy $0\\le A_b\\le W$. Their cosine matrices may be complex Hermitian. For every normalized bosonic ground vector, $$\\label{mom:local-covariance-formula}\n \\frac1V\\langle\\mathrm d\\Gamma(A)\\rangle\n       =\\frac1{v_b}\\sum_{p\\ne0}(A_b)_{pp}F_\\alpha(p)+o(1),\n \\qquad (A_b)_{pp}=\\langle u_p,A_bu_p\\rangle.$$ The error is uniform in the ground vector and in all the indicated operators, which may depend on the scales. The same formula and uniformity hold when $A_b=WC_bW$ and $C_b$ is any complex contraction, identical in local coordinates. Both assertions hold uniformly for simultaneous translates of the nested partitions.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-en-observable",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E=B$, $n$ satisfy <a href=\"#en:middle-sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"en:middle-sector\">[en:middle-sector]</a>, and $|z|^2\\le2n$. For $0\\le A_b\\le W$ and an excitation vector $\\xi$ in the form domain of $n_+$, put $$\\mathcal Q_z(\\xi)=\\sum_{p\\ne0}\\widetilde D_p(z)\\|b_p(z)\\xi\\|^2,\n \\qquad\n T_A(z)=\\sum_{p\\ne0}(A_b)_{pp}|V_p(z)|^2,$$ where $a_p=U_pb_p+V_pb_p^*$ is the inverse of <a href=\"#en:bogoliubov\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"en:bogoliubov\">[en:bogoliubov]</a>, with the phase of $z$ restored. Then $0\\le T_A(z)\\le Cv_b$ and $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left|\\langle\\xi,\\mathrm d\\Gamma(A_b)\\xi\\rangle\n                   -T_A(z)\\|\\xi\\|^2\\right|\n &\\le C(1+R^2)\\mathcal Q_z(\\xi)\\nonumber\\\\\n &\\quad+C\\{(1+R^2)\\mathcal Q_z(\\xi)\n                            T_A(z)\\|\\xi\\|^2\\}^{1/2}.\n \\label{en:observable-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constant is independent of $A_b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-en-observable",

        "exact_quote": "The constants in Definition <a href=\"#lp:certificate\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lp:certificate\">18</a> can be chosen so that $$\\label{lp:good-probability-bounds}\n \\mathbb P\\{v\\text{ is bad}\\}\\leq C(x)D^{-1-1/8},\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb P\\{A\\text{ consists of bad sites}\\}\\leq p^{|A|}$$ for every site $v$ and every set $A$ of distinct sites.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lp-collision",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the exposed data in a good neighborhood. Let $k$ be a deterministic path satisfying its individual cutoffs, and let $i\\ne k$ have the eligible endpoints. Under $B_i$, the probability that the trial satisfies its own cutoffs and comes within distance $r$ of path $k$ is at most $\\beta_{ik}$. For two legs pinned at a prescribed midpoint $y$, with $|y-m_i|\\leq8b$ and $d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))>s$, the bound has the additional term $$\\frac{b_D}{s\\vee d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))}.$$ If $y$ is instead uniform in a unit cell at a fixed bounded distance from $m_i$, the integrated collision probability, including failure of this midpoint separation, is at most $C\\beta_{ik}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lp-collision",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the exposed data in a good neighborhood. Let $k$ be a deterministic path satisfying its individual cutoffs, and let $i\\ne k$ have the eligible endpoints. For two legs pinned at a prescribed midpoint $y$, with $|y-m_i|\\leq8b$ and $d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))>s$, the bound has the additional term $$\\frac{b_D}{s\\vee d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))}.$$ If $y$ is instead uniform in a unit cell at a fixed bounded distance from $m_i$, the integrated collision probability, including failure of this midpoint separation, is at most $C\\beta_{ik}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix the exposed data in a good neighborhood. Let $k$ be a deterministic path satisfying its individual cutoffs, and let $i\\ne k$ have the eligible endpoints. Under $B_i$, the probability that the trial satisfies its own cutoffs and comes within distance $r$ of path $k$ is at most $\\beta_{ik}$. For two legs pinned at a prescribed midpoint $y$, with $|y-m_i|\\leq8b$ and $d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))>s$, the bound has the additional term $$\\frac{b_D}{s\\vee d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))}.$$ If $y$ is instead uniform in a unit cell at a fixed bounded distance from $m_i$, the integrated collision probability, including failure of this midpoint separation, is at most $C\\beta_{ik}$.",
      "explanation": "A trial bridge collides with a fixed allowed path with small probability.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lp-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix good data in all neighborhoods met by the following trials. In the first three assertions existing unexposed paths may take any values satisfying their individual cutoffs, while the frozen paths remain those of the fixed data. The fourth assertion instead concerns an obstacle collection satisfying the full-path bounds. For all sufficiently large $D$, these assertions hold also when an arbitrary subset of avoidance checks is omitted.*\n\n1.  *An ordinary trial $B_i$, required to satisfy its own cutoffs and avoid the frozen paths and all indicated other unexposed paths, succeeds with probability at least $3/4$.*\n\n2.  *A trial formed by choosing a uniform midpoint in a unit cell contained in $B(m_i,8b)$ and at an absolute constant distance from its mean, then choosing independent bridge legs, has success probability at least $3/4$. It may also be required to have midpoint distance greater than $s$ from every opponent.*\n\n3.  *Two such trials in two copies, using a common unit cell meeting the preceding conditions for both labels, with a common uniform midpoint and independent legs conditional on that midpoint, simultaneously succeed with probability at least $1/2$. Each is checked against the previously present paths in its own copy; the pair is not checked against itself across the copies.*\n\n4.  *Suppose the entire obstacle collection satisfies the motion and count bounds defining the full-path event. A trial pinned at any point within $8b$ of its mean and more than $s$ from every obstacle midpoint succeeds with probability at least $3/4$. This remains valid after adding or substituting a fixed number of individually allowed obstacles, provided the indicated endpoint and midpoint separations hold.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lp-insertion",
          "exact_quote": "Fix good data in all neighborhoods met by the following trials. In the first three assertions existing unexposed paths may take any values satisfying their individual cutoffs, while the frozen paths remain those of the fixed data. The fourth assertion instead concerns an obstacle collection satisfying the full-path bounds. For all sufficiently large $D$, these assertions hold also when an arbitrary subset of avoidance checks is omitted.*\n\n1.  *An ordinary trial $B_i$, required to satisfy its own cutoffs and avoid the frozen paths and all indicated other unexposed paths, succeeds with probability at least $3/4$.*\n\n2.  *A trial formed by choosing a uniform midpoint in a unit cell contained in $B(m_i,8b)$ and at an absolute constant distance from its mean, then choosing independent bridge legs, has success probability at least $3/4$. It may also be required to have midpoint distance greater than $s$ from every opponent.*\n\n3.  *Two such trials in two copies, using a common unit cell meeting the preceding conditions for both labels, with a common uniform midpoint and independent legs conditional on that midpoint, simultaneously succeed with probability at least $1/2$. Each is checked against the previously present paths in its own copy; the pair is not checked against itself across the copies.*\n\n4.  *Suppose the entire obstacle collection satisfies the motion and count bounds defining the full-path event. A trial pinned at any point within $8b$ of its mean and more than $s$ from every obstacle midpoint succeeds with probability at least $3/4$. This remains valid after adding or substituting a fixed number of individually allowed obstacles, provided the indicated endpoint and midpoint separations hold.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lp-trial-density",
        "exact_quote": "Fix good data and a set of route labels $S\\subset\\mathcal I$ with a bounded number of labels per site. In a product of their free bridge probabilities, condition on individual cutoffs and any selection of avoidance checks, including checks against fixed outside paths. The paths with labels in $\\mathcal I\\setminus S$ need only satisfy their individual cutoffs; the frozen paths remain those of the fixed good data. Every specified $q$ labels have joint marginal density at most $C^q$ relative to the product of their $B_i$. The conclusion also holds conditional on the values of all other labels. A uniform-midpoint insertion has the additional density expense $CD^{Cx}$ for each inserted marginal, or in an augmented integral retaining the common midpoint, and replacing one free midpoint density by that of a neighboring label has expense <a href=\"#lp:midpoint-ratio\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"lp:midpoint-ratio\">[lp:midpoint-ratio]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For adjacent $d$-cells on the retained data event one can choose <a href=\"#palm:label-list\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"palm:label-list\">[palm:label-list]</a> of length $m\\le C_x$, with $C_x=O(x^{-1/2})$, and construct two path copies with these properties:*\n\n1.  *All unselected paths are the same as in the original sample. The left path of $i$ and the right path of $j$ are also unchanged.*\n\n2.  *Both copies satisfy every path constraint defining the conditional law at the given datum.*\n\n3.  *For $1\\le k\\le m$, the left midpoint of $i_k$ equals the right midpoint of $i_{k-1}$. Thus, after deleting $i$ on the left and $j$ on the right, the midpoint configurations agree as unordered lists.*\n\n4.  *The construction is a probability kernel conditional on the original sample and the route choices.*\n\n*Consequently, weighting by <a href=\"#palm:two-tag-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"palm:two-tag-weight\">[palm:two-tag-weight]</a>, averaging all choices, and ordering the common remaining midpoint list uniformly defines a single finite measure $\\nu_{v,w}$ on the remaining particles.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-palm-likelihood",
        "exact_quote": "The left-output likelihood, with the weight $h_{w,j}$ included but the divisor $T_w$ omitted, is, on admissible reference values, $$\\label{palm:likelihood-formula}\n L_S(z;\\xi)\n  =\\int a_S(o;\\xi)h_{w,j}(o)\n          k_S(o;\\xi,z)\\prod_{l\\in S}B_l(\\mathrm do_l).$$ It satisfies $$\\label{palm:centered-identity}\n F_S=L_S-h_{w,j},\\qquad\n       \\mathbb E_\\lambda[F_S\\mid\\omega_{S^c}]=0.$$ These assertions hold after removing any specified collection of outside checks, provided the same removals are used in the reference, the original-path integral, and the insertion kernels.           "source_id": "PM-267.2"

      ],
      "exact_statement": "The left-output likelihood, with the weight $h_{w,j}$ included but the divisor $T_w$ omitted, is, on admissible reference values, $$\\label{palm:likelihood-formula}\n L_S(z;\\xi)\n  =\\int a_S(o;\\xi)h_{w,j}(o)\n          k_S(o;\\xi,z)\\prod_{l\\in S}B_l(\\mathrm do_l).$$ It satisfies $$\\label{palm:centered-identity}\n F_S=L_S-h_{w,j},\\qquad\n       \\mathbb E_\\lambda[F_S\\mid\\omega_{S^c}]=0.$$ These assertions hold after removing any specified collection of outside checks, provided the same removals are used in the reference, the original-path integral, and the insertion kernels. Moreover, $$\\label{palm:local-likelihood-sup}\n                    0\\le L_S\\le C_xD^{C\\sqrt x}.$$",
      "explanation": "The conditional normalizer cancels, the centered likelihood has zero conditional mean and is bounded.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-palm-two-switches",
        "exact_quote": "Write $\\beta_{lk}$ for the collision bound in Lemma <a href=\"#lp:collision\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lp:collision\">20</a>, and set $$\\beta_* = C_xD^{-19/100}.$$ Suppose either that the four changed sets are disjoint, or that they have exactly one independent label identification. After increasing $D$ the following bounds hold for their centered likelihoods, integrated against the conditional reference on their union. Finite differences act on the entire admissibility numerator and its likelihood factors; the original conditional-reference normalizer is held fixed.*\n\n1.  *A finite difference that switches one block-pair interaction has absolute integral at most $C_xD^{C\\sqrt x}$ times the sum of the $\\beta_{lk}$ for the finitely many label pairs checked across that interaction. In particular it is at most $C_xD^{C\\sqrt x}\\beta_*$.*\n\n2.  *If all four lists have disjoint changed sets, a mixed difference in two distinct block-pair interactions has absolute integral at most $C_xD^{C\\sqrt x}$ times a finite sum of products $\\beta_{lk}\\beta_{l'k'}$, with one checked pair from each interaction.*\n\n*The bounds are valid if additional switches have already been fixed on or off. The number of summands is bounded by a constant depending only on $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-palm-fourth",
        "exact_quote": "For adjacent $d$-cells $v,w$, let an overbar denote the average over the route and interior label choices at fixed tags and datum.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rt-routes",
        "exact_quote": "There are $p_0,\\kappa,c,C>0$ and $R_0$ with the following property. Suppose $R\\ge R_0$, $K\\ge C_0R$, and <a href=\"#rt:bad-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rt:bad-law\">[rt:bad-law]</a> holds with $p\\le p_0$. Put $h=\\lfloor R^{1/2}\\rfloor$. There is an event $\\mathcal E$, determined by $\\mathcal D,z,Q$, on which the construction below gives simple good nearest-neighbor routes $\\pi_y$ from $z$ to every $y\\in Q$. Conditional on the datum, routes are sampled using fresh independent random variables. The event includes the requirement that the $10R$-neighborhoods of $z$ and $Q$ contain no bad sites.*\n\n\n\n*For a route $\\pi=(v_0,\\ldots,v_m)$ starting at $z$, set $$a(\\pi)=\\max\\{i:d_\\infty(v_i,z)\\le h\\},\\qquad\n \\pi^{\\rm ch}=\\{v_i:i>a(\\pi)\\}.$$ Its silent portion $(v_0,\\ldots,v_{a(\\pi)})$ has at most $Ch$ vertices. The analogous silent portion read backwards from $y$ also has at most $Ch$ vertices, and the two portions are disjoint. Let $\\pi^{\\rm ch,q}$ consist of vertices whose path index is within $q$ of an index in $\\pi^{\\rm ch}$. For independently sampled routes $\\pi_y,\\pi_{y'}$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n J&=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y}\\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}}\n       \\mathbf1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}},\\\\\n J_*&=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\mathbf1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every fixed pair $y,y'\\in Q$, $$\\label{rt:exponential}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\!\\left[\n  \\mathbf1_{\\mathcal E}\\,\n  \\mathbb E_{\\rm routes}(e^{\\kappa J}\\mid\\mathcal D)\\right]\\le C.$$ For every deterministic probability mass function $\\omega$ on $Q\\times Q$ satisfying $\\omega(y,y')\\le C_\\omega R^{-6}$, $$\\label{rt:rare}\n \\sum_{y,y'\\in Q}\\omega(y,y')\\,\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\!\\left[\n  \\mathbf1_{\\mathcal E}\\,\n  \\mathbb P_{\\rm routes}(J_*>0\\mid\\mathcal D)\\right]\n \\le CC_\\omega R^{-c_g},\\qquad\n c_g=\\frac12\\left(\\frac{\\log2}{\\log3}-\\frac12\\right)>0.$$ The same conclusions hold with a common terminal endpoint, reading the routes in reverse order.*\n\n\n*If $\\mathcal M$ denotes just the stated bad-free margin event, then $$\\label{rt:rejection}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathcal M\\setminus\\mathcal E)\n \\le K^3(Ap)^{\\lfloor K/(10(r_c+2))\\rfloor}\n       +CR^3e^{-cR}.$$ Here $A$ is fixed and $p_0$ is chosen so that $Ap_0<1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rt-routes",

Conditional on the datum, routes are sampled using fresh independent random variables. The event includes the requirement that the $10R$-neighborhoods of $z$ and $Q$ contain no bad sites.*\n\n\n\n*For a route $\\pi=(v_0,\\ldots,v_m)$ starting at $z$, set $$a(\\pi)=\\max\\{i:d_\\infty(v_i,z)\\le h\\},\\qquad\n \\pi^{\\rm ch}=\\{v_i:i>a(\\pi)\\}.$$ Its silent portion $(v_0,\\ldots,v_{a(\\pi)})$ has at most $Ch$ vertices. Let $\\pi^{\\rm ch,q}$ consist of vertices whose path index is within $q$ of an index in $\\pi^{\\rm ch}$. For independently sampled routes $\\pi_y,\\pi_{y'}$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n J&=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y}\\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}}\n       \\mathbf1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}},\\\\\n J_*&=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\mathbf1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every fixed pair $y,y'\\in Q$, $$\\label{rt:exponential}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\!\\left[\n  \\mathbf1_{\\mathcal E}\\,\n  \\mathbb E_{\\rm routes}(e^{\\kappa J}\\mid\\mathcal D)\\right]\\le C.$$ For every deterministic probability mass function $\\omega$ on $Q\\times Q$ satisfying $\\omega(y,y')\\le C_\\omega R^{-6}$, $$\\label{rt:rare}\n \\sum_{y,y'\\in Q}\\omega(y,y')\\,\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\!\\left[\n  \\mathbf1_{\\mathcal E}\\,\n  \\mathbb P_{\\rm routes}(J_*>0\\mid\\mathcal D)\\right]\n \\le CC_\\omega R^{-c_g},\\qquad\n c_g=\\frac12\\left(\\frac{\\log2}{\\log3}-\\frac12\\right)>0.$$ The same conclusions hold with a common terminal endpoint, reading the routes in reverse order.*\n\n\n*If $\\mathcal M$ denotes just the stated bad-free margin event, then $$\\label{rt:rejection}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathcal M\\setminus\\mathcal E)\n \\le K^3(Ap)^{\\lfloor K/(10(r_c+2))\\rfloor}\n       +CR^3e^{-cR}.$$ Here $A$ is fixed and $p_0$ is chosen so that $Ap_0<1$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rt-prediction",
        "exact_quote": "Put $\\sigma=\\log2/\\log3$. For every integer $T\\ge1$ there is an integer-valued process $(S_j)_{j=0}^T$, with $S_0=0$ and $|S_{j+1}-S_j|=1$, such that $$\\label{rt:prediction-bound}\n \\sup_{x\\in\\mathbb Z}\n \\mathbb P(S_{u+k}=x\\mid S_0,\\ldots,S_u)\n \\le 6^\\sigma k^{-\\sigma}\n \\quad(k\\ge1,\\ u+k\\le T).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rt-prediction",
          "exact_quote": "Put $\\sigma=\\log2/\\log3$. For every integer $T\\ge1$ there is an integer-valued process $(S_j)_{j=0}^T$, with $S_0=0$ and $|S_{j+1}-S_j|=1$, such that $$\\label{rt:prediction-bound}\n \\sup_{x\\in\\mathbb Z}\n \\mathbb P(S_{u+k}=x\\mid S_0,\\ldots,S_u)\n \\le 6^\\sigma k^{-\\sigma}\n \\quad(k\\ge1,\\ u+k\\le T).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $\\sigma=\\log2/\\log3$. For every integer $T\\ge1$ there is an integer-valued process $(S_j)_{j=0}^T$, with $S_0=0$ and $|S_{j+1}-S_j|=1$, such that $$\\label{rt:prediction-bound}\n \\sup_{x\\in\\mathbb Z}\n \\mathbb P(S_{u+k}=x\\mid S_0,\\ldots,S_u)\n \\le 6^\\sigma k^{-\\sigma}\n \\quad(k\\ge1,\\ u+k\\le T).$$",
      "explanation": "A nearest-neighbour integer process has conditional atoms decaying as a power of the lag.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rt-ordered",
        "exact_quote": "At each time $s\\in\\{0,\\ldots,T\\}$, let $A_s(l)$ be a union of at most two deterministic squares of maximum radius $l$, with arbitrary centers, in the transverse torus. There is a constant $C_1$, independent of their centers and of $T\\le K/2$, such that $$\\label{rt:ordered-bound}\n \\sum_{0\\le s_1<\\cdots<s_j\\le T}\n \\mathbb P\\{X_{s_i}\\in A_{s_i}(l_i),\\ 1\\le i\\le j\\}\n \\le \\prod_{i=1}^jC_1(1+l_i)^2.$$ In particular, for $a_l\\ge0$, if $C_1\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2<1$, then $$\\label{rt:ordered-product}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{s=0}^T\n \\left(1+\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l\\mathbf1_{\\{X_s\\in A_s(l)\\}}\\right)\n \\le \\left(1-C_1\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2\\right)^{-1}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rt-ordered",
          "exact_quote": "At each time $s\\in\\{0,\\ldots,T\\}$, let $A_s(l)$ be a union of at most two deterministic squares of maximum radius $l$, with arbitrary centers, in the transverse torus. There is a constant $C_1$, independent of their centers and of $T\\le K/2$, such that $$\\label{rt:ordered-bound}\n \\sum_{0\\le s_1<\\cdots<s_j\\le T}\n \\mathbb P\\{X_{s_i}\\in A_{s_i}(l_i),\\ 1\\le i\\le j\\}\n \\le \\prod_{i=1}^jC_1(1+l_i)^2.$$ In particular, for $a_l\\ge0$, if $C_1\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2<1$, then $$\\label{rt:ordered-product}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{s=0}^T\n \\left(1+\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l\\mathbf1_{\\{X_s\\in A_s(l)\\}}\\right)\n \\le \\left(1-C_1\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2\\right)^{-1}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At each time $s\\in\\{0,\\ldots,T\\}$, let $A_s(l)$ be a union of at most two deterministic squares of maximum radius $l$, with arbitrary centers, in the transverse torus. There is a constant $C_1$, independent of their centers and of $T\\le K/2$, such that $$\\label{rt:ordered-bound}\n \\sum_{0\\le s_1<\\cdots<s_j\\le T}\n \\mathbb P\\{X_{s_i}\\in A_{s_i}(l_i),\\ 1\\le i\\le j\\}\n \\le \\prod_{i=1}^jC_1(1+l_i)^2.$$ In particular, for $a_l\\ge0$, if $C_1\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2<1$, then $$\\label{rt:ordered-product}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{s=0}^T\n \\left(1+\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l\\mathbf1_{\\{X_s\\in A_s(l)\\}}\\right)\n \\le \\left(1-C_1\\sum_{l\\ge0}a_l(1+l)^2\\right)^{-1}.$$",
      "explanation": "Ordered hitting probabilities of moving targets factor into geometric products.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-long-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu(\\mathrm dx)$ be a probability measure of latent variables, followed by a finite ordered list of subprobability kernels.         "source_id": "PM-267.2"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Good site** ([source](#stmt-lp-certificate)): A unit-cell site is good if all of the following conditions hold. 1. It has at least $c f_0D$ interior labels. In its $R_*$ neighborhood, each unit cell has at most $CD$ endpoints at either time and at most $Cf_0D$ flagged endpoints at either time. 2. At either endpoint time, every ball $B(z,h)$ in the neighborhood, with $r\leq h\leq1$, satisfies $$\label{lp:flagged-count} \#\{i:\ i\text{ flagged},\ Y_i\in B(z,h)\} \leq C(f_0Dh^3+D^{1/100}),$$ and the same assertion holds for $Z_i$. 3. Every frozen path visiting the relevant neighborhood satisfies <a href="#lp:cutoffs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lp:cutoffs">[lp:cutoffs]</a> and has lifted diameter at most $Cb$. At each mesh time $q$, and for either adjacent mesh interval $J$, the frozen paths satisfy $$\label{lp:frozen-count} \sum_{k\notin\mathcal I:\ \omega_k(q)\in B(z,h)} (1+A_J(\omega_k))^3 \leq C(Dh^3+D^{1/100})\qquad(r\leq h\leq1).$$ The unweighted version is also required at endpoints and at time zero. All ball tests may be made on finite spatial nets with dyadic radii, using enlarged balls and fixed margins. The stated inequalities for arbitrary balls then follow by covering. The constants $c,C$ are fixed before taking $D$ large. Write $\mathcal B$ for the set of bad sites.
- **Remaining-excitation projection** ([source](#stmt-asm-infrared)): Put $Q_R=P_R-P_0$.
- **Close-neighbor distance** ([source](#stmt-set-close-tail)): For each particle label $i$ let $d_i(X)=\min_{k\ne i}d_K(x_i,x_k)$, with the value $+\infty$ if $N=1$.
- **Unpredictability exponent** ([source](#stmt-rt-prediction)): Put $\sigma=\log2/\log3$.
- **Phase factor** ([source](#en:bogoliubov)): For $z\ne0$ put $\zeta_z=z/\|z\|$, and set $\zeta_0=1$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-ground-space** [claim](#stmt-set-ground-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-set-ground-space): Zero extension, compact Sobolev embedding and root-mean-square symmetrization -> Compact resolvent and equal lowest energies
- **step-rough-bound** [claim](#stmt-en-rough) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-rough): Radial scattering replacement, Temple inequality, convexity and Jensen -> Rough box lower bound
- **step-localization** [claim](#stmt-en-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-localization): Three smooth cutoffs in excitation number -> Cutoff form error
- **step-small-gap** [claim](#stmt-en-small-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-small-gap): Bogoliubov identity, square completion, cubic term bounds -> Quadratic-cubic lower bound
- **step-cosine-correction** [claim](#stmt-en-cosine-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-cosine-correction): Exact cosine expansion and weighted Cauchy-Schwarz -> Absorbed coefficient correction
- **step-sharp-bound** [claim](#stmt-en-sharp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-cosine-correction-2): Symmetrization and coherent resolution of the zero mode -> Box energy with retained quasiparticle term
- **step-surplus** [claim](#stmt-en-surplus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-surplus): Sum localized bounds and subtract thermodynamic upper bound -> Surplus control
- **step-covariance** [claim](#stmt-mom-local-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-en-observable-2): Coherent trace reduction and density concentration -> Uniform local covariance
- **step-good-sites** [claim](#stmt-lp-good-probability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-good-probability): Gaussian tails, visitor moments, exponential Markov -> Rare bad sites
- **step-insertion** [claim](#stmt-lp-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-insertion): Sum collision bounds and weighted frozen counts -> Insertion probabilities
- **step-local-comparison** [claim](#stmt-palm-local-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-palm-augmented-comparison-2): Two-copy coupling, switch expansion, Jensen projection -> Local comparison of deletion measures
- **step-routes** [claim](#stmt-rt-routes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rt-prediction): Folded unpredictable walk with detours around bad components -> Routes with rare encounters
- **step-distant-comparison** [claim](#stmt-long-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-long-augmented-moments-2): Common measure along routes with second-moment control -> Distant deletion measures agree
- **step-infrared** [claim](#stmt-asm-infrared) / [proof](#proof-stmt-asm-infrared): Variance bound plus RMS interpolation and positive-part decomposition -> Vanishing intermediate excitations
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mom-characteristic-2): Characteristic integrals, tightness and rescaling to physical units -> Weak convergence of momentum distribution

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-ternary-tree-atom-bound**: For the ternary-tree spin process of Lemma 33, with leaf sums as the walk, every conditional point probability at lag k given the past is at most 6^\sigma k^{-\sigma}, for all histories and all targets with u+k not exceeding the horizon.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-unit-steps**: Reuse the atom bound for a walk whose increments are not plus or minus one, or that does not start at zero. -> The bound is proved only for the ternary-tree spin construction; other processes can have large atoms.
- **anti-lag-zero-or-beyond-horizon**: Quote the decay in k for lag zero or for times beyond the horizon T. -> The bound is stated only for positive lags inside the horizon; at lag zero the conditional atom is one and no decay holds.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Lemma 33 gives an explicit finite construction (ternary tree with inherited and fresh fair spins); for small depth the exact conditional atoms can be enumerated and compared with the stated bound. The headline asymptotic theorem is not finite and is not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: one integer m (tree depth) on stdin
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= m <= 3 so that all fresh-spin assignments can be enumerated
# OUTPUTS: PASS if every exact conditional atom of the leaf-sum process obeys the stated power bound for all u,k with u+k<=3^m, else FAIL
# TIME: O(2^n * T^2) with T=3^m and n=(T-1)/2
# SPACE: O(2^n * T)
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer counts; the bound uses double-precision powers with 1e-12 slack
# FAILURE: m outside the range raises ValueError (nonzero exit)
import sys, math
from itertools import product

SIGMA = math.log(2) / math.log(3)


def leaves(depth, spin, it):
    if depth == 0:
        return [spin]
    a = leaves(depth - 1, spin, it)
    b = leaves(depth - 1, spin, it)
    f = next(it)
    c = leaves(depth - 1, f, it)
    return a + b + c


m = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
if m < 0 or m > 3:
    raise ValueError("depth out of range")
T = 3 ** m
n = (T - 1) // 2
seqs = []
for bits in product((-1, 1), repeat=n):
    it = iter(bits)
    sp = leaves(m, 1, it)
    S = [0]
    for x in sp:
        S.append(S[-1] + x)
    seqs.append(S)
ok = True
for u in range(T):
    groups = {}
    for S in seqs:
        groups.setdefault(tuple(S[:u + 1]), []).append(S)
    for g in groups.values():
        for k in range(1, T - u + 1):
            c = {}
            for S in g:
                v = S[u + k]
                c[v] = c.get(v, 0) + 1
            p = max(c.values()) / len(g)
            if p > (6.0 / k) ** SIGMA + 1e-12:
                ok = False
print("PASS" if ok else "FAIL")
```

        "observed_exit": 1,
