<a id="pm-h-49"></a>
#### The Fourier transform of the limiting density

Each cut translation acts separately on the $B$-boxes. We may therefore apply Proposition <a href="#mom:local-covariance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mom:local-covariance">16</a> to it. The remaining calculation identifies the Neumann-mode trace with the Fourier transform of $F_\alpha$, defined in <a href="#en:limiting-V" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:limiting-V">[en:limiting-V]</a>.

<a id="stmt-mom-characteristic"></a>
<div id="mom:characteristic" class="proposition">

<a id="mom:characteristic-limit"></a>
**Proposition 49** (Momentum characteristic integrals). *For every fixed $H<\infty$, $$\label{mom:characteristic-limit}
 \int e^{ih\cdot p}\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m_\Phi(p)
     =\int_{\mathbb R^3}e^{ih\cdot p}F_\alpha(p)
                          \frac{\mathrm dp}{(2\pi)^3}+o(1),
 \qquad |h|\le H.$$ The error is uniform in $h$ and in normalized, possibly complex, ground vectors, with the iterated-limit convention of Section <a href="#set:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:section">2</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mom-characteristic"><summary>Proof of stmt-mom-characteristic</summary>

<a id="mom:local-translation-trace"></a>
*Proof.* Fix a shifted partition $\mathcal C$. In local coordinates $[0,B)^3$, the restriction of $T_h^{\mathcal C}$ is the contraction $$(C_{b,h}g)(y)=\mathbf 1_{[0,B)^3}(y+h)g(y+h).$$ It is identical in every box. Write $W$ for the restriction of $W_R$ to a box, and $e_p$ for its normalized Neumann cosine mode with $p\in(\pi/B)\mathbb N_0^3$. Proposition <a href="#mom:local-covariance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mom:local-covariance">16</a>, including its complex-contraction assertion, gives $$\label{mom:local-translation-trace}
 \frac1V\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(W_RT_h^{\mathcal C}W_R)\rangle_\Phi
  =\frac1{B^3}\sum_{p\ne0}
        \langle e_p,WC_{b,h}We_p\rangle F_\alpha(p)+o(1).$$ The estimate applies uniformly to these shifted grids: simultaneous translation maps the ground space unitarily to itself, and the local bound is uniform over its normalized vectors.

<a id="mom:Neumann-diagonal"></a>
We compute the diagonal on a fixed annulus $0<\delta\le|p|\le J$. At least one coordinate has $|p_i|\ge\delta/\sqrt3$. The mean of its cosine over an $R$-interval is bounded by $2/(R|p_i|)$, whereas each other cosine mean has absolute value at most one. Including the Neumann normalization factors and summing the squared cell means gives $$\|(1-W)e_p\|\le\frac{C}{\delta R}.$$ Since $C_{b,h}$ is a contraction, $$\label{mom:Neumann-diagonal}
 \langle e_p,WC_{b,h}We_p\rangle
   =\prod_{i=1}^3\cos(p_i h_i)
                  +O\left(\frac1{\delta R}+\frac HB\right).$$ To verify the translation term, extend the cosine formula for $e_p$ to $\mathbb R^3$. Removing the clipping changes its integral by $O(H/B)$: the boundary layer has relative volume $O(H/B)$ and $|e_p(y)e_p(y+h)|\le C/B^3$. On the full box the integral factors. For $p_i>0$, the sine–cosine integral over $[0,B]$ is zero because $p_iB\in\pi\mathbb N$; the cosine-square integral gives $\cos(p_i h_i)$. For $p_i=0$ the factor is one.

<a id="mom:profile-majorant"></a>
Uniformly for $\alpha\in I_\alpha$, the explicit formula gives $$\label{mom:profile-majorant}
 F_\alpha(p)\le C\min(|p|^{-1},|p|^{-4}).$$ The normalized Neumann-lattice tails in <a href="#en:lattice-tails" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:lattice-tails">[en:lattice-tails]</a> therefore bound the contributions of $0<|p|\le\delta$ and $|p|\ge J$ by $C\delta^2$ and $C/J$, respectively, uniformly in $B$, for $0<\delta<1<J$. The same bounds hold for the corresponding integrals.

We can now truncate <a href="#mom:local-translation-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:local-translation-trace">[mom:local-translation-trace]</a> to a fixed annulus, use <a href="#mom:Neumann-diagonal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:Neumann-diagonal">[mom:Neumann-diagonal]</a>, and take Riemann sums. The Neumann mesh has density $\pi^{-3}$ in the nonnegative octant. On the annulus the integrands are bounded and equicontinuous for $|h|\le H$ and $\alpha\in I_\alpha$; continuous cutoffs at its edges may be used. Letting $\delta\downarrow0$ and $J\uparrow\infty$ after these estimates yields, uniformly in $h$ and $\mathcal C$, <a id="mom:octant-Fourier"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \frac1V\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(W_RT_h^{\mathcal C}W_R)\rangle_\Phi
 &=\frac1{\pi^3}\int_{[0,\infty)^3}
       F_\alpha(p)\prod_i\cos(p_i h_i)\,\mathrm dp+o(1)\notag\\
 &=\int_{\mathbb R^3}F_\alpha(p)e^{ih\cdot p}
                          \frac{\mathrm dp}{(2\pi)^3}+o(1).
 \label{mom:octant-Fourier}
\end{aligned}$$ The last equality uses reflection symmetry in each coordinate. Averaging over $\mathcal C$ and applying <a href="#mom:remove-coarse" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:remove-coarse">[mom:remove-coarse]</a> and <a href="#mom:boundary-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:boundary-cost">[mom:boundary-cost]</a> proves <a href="#mom:characteristic-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:characteristic-limit">[mom:characteristic-limit]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-50"></a>
#### Physical units and bounded continuous tests

The characteristic integrals include the total mass by taking $h=0$. Fourier inversion will first give compact-test convergence. Together with total mass convergence and positivity, this controls the momentum tails and allows arbitrary bounded continuous test functions.

<details id="proof-stmt-mom-characteristic-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-mom-characteristic</summary>

<a id="asm:physical-scale"></a>
*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Fix $a>0$. For a sufficiently small density $\rho>0$, put $\eta=\rho a^3$ and $$\label{asm:physical-scale}
 s_* =\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_0}{\rho a}},\qquad
 D_* =\rho s_*^3=\frac{\alpha_0^{3/2}}{\sqrt\eta}.$$ Choose $R,B$ from this $D_*$ by <a href="#set:nested-lengths" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="set:nested-lengths">[set:nested-lengths]</a>. For any thermodynamic sequence $N_k,L_k\to\infty$ with $N_k/L_k^3\to\rho$, let $K_k$ be a positive multiple of $B$ nearest to $L_k/s_*$. Set $$s_k=L_k/K_k,\qquad r_k=a/s_k,\qquad
 D_k=N_k/K_k^3,\qquad \alpha_k=D_kr_k.$$ Then $K_k\to\infty$, $s_k\to s_*$, $D_k\to D_*$, and $\alpha_k\to\alpha_0$. Thus all the nested partitions and parameter conditions hold on the tail of every such sequence.

<a id="asm:gas-identity"></a>
The unitary change of variables is <a id="asm:unitary-scaling"></a>$$\label{asm:unitary-scaling}
 (U_k\Psi)(y_1,\ldots,y_{N_k})
       =s_k^{3N_k/2}\Psi(s_ky_1,\ldots,s_ky_{N_k}).$$ It maps the allowed sets and form domains to their scaled versions, with $q_{K_k,r_k}[U_k\Psi]=s_k^2q_{L_k,a}[\Psi]$, and hence maps ground eigenspaces unitarily. The corresponding one-particle unitary sends a physical plane wave of momentum $\kappa$ to the scaled one of momentum $p=s_k\kappa$, preserving its occupation. Also $$\label{asm:gas-identity}
 D_kr_k^3=(N_k/L_k^3)a^3\longrightarrow\eta,
 \qquad D_k\sqrt\eta\longrightarrow\alpha_0^{3/2}.$$

Suppress $k$ temporarily. For a normalized physical ground vector $\Psi$, put $\Phi=U\Psi$ and <a id="mom:physical-measure"></a>$$\label{mom:physical-measure}
 \lambda=s\sqrt{8\pi\rho a},\qquad
 \sigma_\Psi=\frac1{D\sqrt\eta}
                  (p\mapsto p/\lambda)_*\mathfrak m_\Phi.$$ The normalized momentum sum in <a href="#intro:main-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:main-limit">[intro:main-limit]</a>, evaluated in the pure state $|\Psi\rangle\langle\Psi|$, is $\int f\,\mathrm d\sigma_\Psi$. In the inner limit, $\lambda\to\lambda_0:=\sqrt{8\pi\alpha_0}$. The target measure $\sigma_0:=\nu_{\mathrm{Bog}}$ from <a href="#intro:profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:profile">[intro:profile]</a> satisfies <a id="mom:target-measure"></a>$$\label{mom:target-measure}
 \sigma_0=(p\mapsto p/\lambda_0)_*
       \left(\alpha_0^{-3/2}F_{\alpha_0}(p)
                                    \frac{\mathrm dp}{(2\pi)^3}\right).$$ This follows by substituting $p=\sqrt{8\pi\alpha_0}\,t$ in the explicit formula <a href="#en:limiting-V" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="en:limiting-V">[en:limiting-V]</a>.

<a id="mom:physical-characteristic"></a>
Apply Proposition <a href="#mom:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mom:characteristic">49</a> at $h/\lambda$ and use <a href="#asm:gas-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:gas-identity">[asm:gas-identity]</a>. Since $\lambda$ stays bounded away from zero, we obtain $$\label{mom:physical-characteristic}
 \int e^{ih\cdot t}\,\mathrm d\sigma_\Psi(t)
       =\int e^{ih\cdot t}\,\mathrm d\sigma_0(t)+o(1),$$ uniformly on each compact set of $h$ and uniformly over the ground vectors. Replacing $\alpha,\lambda$ in the continuous integral by their inner limits costs only an inner-limit error: the majorant in <a href="#mom:profile-majorant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:profile-majorant">[mom:profile-majorant]</a> is integrable, and truncation in $p$ makes this replacement uniform on compact $h$-sets. The case $h=0$ gives total mass convergence and, in particular, a uniform mass bound in the iterated limit.

For $g\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3)$, Fourier inversion and its integrable Fourier transform imply $$\int g\,\mathrm d(\sigma_\Psi-\sigma_0)=o(1).$$ Indeed, on a fixed compact Fourier set use <a href="#mom:physical-characteristic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mom:physical-characteristic">[mom:physical-characteristic]</a>; on its complement use the mass bound times the $L^1$ tail of the Fourier transform. Uniform approximation and the same mass bound give this conclusion for all $g\in C_c(\mathbb R^3)$.

Now let $f$ be any bounded continuous real function. Choose $\chi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3)$ with $0\le\chi\le1$ and $\int(1-\chi)\,\mathrm d\sigma_0$ as small as desired. Positivity gives <a id="mom:tightness"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \left|\int f\,\mathrm d(\sigma_\Psi-\sigma_0)\right|
 &\le\left|\int f\chi\,\mathrm d(\sigma_\Psi-\sigma_0)\right|\notag\\
 &\quad+\|f\|_\infty\left\{
       2\int(1-\chi)\,\mathrm d\sigma_0
       +|\sigma_\Psi(\mathbb R^3)-\sigma_0(\mathbb R^3)|
       +\left|\int\chi\,\mathrm d(\sigma_\Psi-\sigma_0)\right|\right\}.
 \label{mom:tightness}
\end{aligned}$$ The compact tests and the mass difference tend to zero in the stated iterated sense. For a prescribed $\varepsilon>0$, first choose $\chi$ and the compact-test approximations, then choose the auxiliary parameters and a lower threshold on $D_*$. Equation <a href="#asm:physical-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="asm:physical-scale">[asm:physical-scale]</a> turns that threshold into $\rho_0(a,f,\varepsilon)>0$. It works for every fixed $0<\rho<\rho_0$ and every density-convergent thermodynamic sequence. The volume threshold may depend on that fixed density and sequence; no convergence rate for $N_k/L_k^3$ is required.

Finally, Lemma <a href="#set:ground-space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="set:ground-space">3</a> makes every finite-volume ground eigenspace finite dimensional. Each state in $\mathcal G_{N,L,a}$ has a spectral decomposition $\Gamma=\sum_b\omega_b|\Psi_b\rangle\langle\Psi_b|$ with normalized ground vectors, $\omega_b\ge0$, and $\sum_b\omega_b=1$. Its momentum measure is $\sum_b\omega_b\sigma_{\Psi_b}$. The absolute error for $f$ is therefore at most the largest pure-state error at that volume. All preceding bounds were uniform in the pure ground vector, so the conclusion holds with the supremum over $\Gamma\in\mathcal G_{N,L,a}$ required by the theorem. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The midpoint pairing, shown schematically for $m = 8$, $a_{0} = 3$ and $a_{1} = 5$. Each link identifies the left midpoint of $i_{k}$ with the right midpoint of $i_{k − 1}$. The middle pairs receive fresh common midpoints. At each end one original trajectory is retained and its partner is pinned through the retained midpoint. After deleting the crossed first left and last right tags, all remaining midpoints are paired. The rows show label order, not geometric positions or paths.",
      "figure_id": "fig:transport",
      "pdf_page": 60,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:transport",
          "exact_quote": "The midpoint pairing, shown schematically for $m=8$, $a_0=3$\n and $a_1=5$.  Each link identifies the left midpoint of $i_k$ with the\n right midpoint of $i_{k-1}$.  The middle pairs receive fresh common\n midpoints.  At each end one original ",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "Schematic diagram showing two horizontal rows of nodes labeled \"Left copy\" (top row) and \"Right copy\" (bottom row). Each row contains 9 node positions labeled $i_0, i_1, i_2, i_3, i_4, i_5, i_6, i_7, i_8$ from left to right.\n- Top row (\"Left copy\"):\n  - $i_0$: open circle with a diagonal cross ($\\times$) through it; labeled above with \"delete\" and \"$i_0$\".\n  - $i_1, i_2$: open circles; labeled above with \"unchanged prefix\", \"$i_1$\", and \"$i_2$\".\n  - $i_3, i_4, i_5, i_6$: solid blue circles; grouped above by a label \"fresh pairs: $k = 3, \\ldots, 6$\" and individual labels \"$i_3$\", \"$i_4$\", \"$i_5$\", \"$i_6$\".\n  - $i_7, i_8$: solid brown/orange circles; labeled above with \"$i_7$\" and \"$i_8$\".\n- Bottom row (\"Right copy\"):\n  - $i_0, i_1$: solid brown/orange circles; labeled below with \"$i_0$\" and \"$i_1$\".\n  - $i_2, i_3, i_4, i_5$: solid blue circles; labeled below with \"$i_2$\", \"$i_3$\", \"$i_4$\", and \"$i_5$\".\n  - $i_6, i_7$: open circles; labeled below with \"$i_6$\", \"$i_7$\", and \"unchanged suffix\".\n  - $i_8$: open circle with a diagonal cross ($\\times$) through it; labeled below with \"$i_8$\" and \"delete\".\n- Edges/links between the two rows:\n  - Dashed lines connect bottom $i_0$ to top $i_1$, bottom $i_1$ to top $i_2$, bottom $i_6$ to top $i_7$, and bottom $i_7$ to top $i_8$.\n  - Solid blue lines connect bottom $i_2$ to top $i_3$, bottom $i_3$ to top $i_4$, bottom $i_4$ to top $i_5$, and bottom $i_5$ to top $i_6$.\n- Legend at the bottom:\n  - Solid line segment labeled \"fresh common midpoint\".\n  - Dashed line segment labeled \"pin to retained midpoint\"."
    },
    {
      "caption": "A one-dimensional section of the shifted partitions, with $B = 4R$ and a fixed pair $y, y + h$. Thin ticks mark the same $R$-grid in every row; thick ticks are the $B$-box faces. In the first row the translation crosses a box face and is cut. In the other three rows it stays inside one box. In general, only $O(H/R+1)$ of the $B/R$ shifts can separate a pair with $|h| ≤ H$. Sandwiching the averaged error by $W_{R}$ makes its expectation cost proportional to the excitation density, as in [mom:boundary-cost].",
      "figure_id": "fig:shifted-grid",
      "pdf_page": 75,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:shifted-grid",
          "exact_quote": "A one-dimensional section of the shifted partitions, with\n $B=4R$ and a fixed pair $y,y+h$. Thin ticks mark the same $R$-grid in\n every row; thick ticks are the $B$-box faces. In the first row the\n translation crosses a box face and is cut.",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=.88cm,y=.8cm,font=\\small]\n \\foreach \\row/\\shift/\\name in {0/0/{0},1/1/{R},2/2/{2R},3/3/{3R}} {\n  \\begin{scope}[yshift=-\\row*0.8cm]\n   \\node[anchor=east] at (-.35,0) {$\\theta=\\name$};\n   \\draw[gray!65] (0,0)--(8,0);\n   \\foreach \\x in {0,...,8} {\\draw[gray!65] (\\x,-.12)--(\\x,.12);}\n   \\ifnum\\row=0\n    \\foreach \\x in {0,4,8} {\\draw[very thick] (\\x,-.29)--(\\x,.29);}\n    \\draw[->,thick,BrickRed] (3.65,.15)--(4.45,.15);\n    \\fill[BrickRed] (3.65,.15) circle (1.5pt);\n    \\fill[BrickRed] (4.45,.15) circle (1.5pt);\n    \\node[anchor=west,BrickRed] at (8.3,0) {cut};\n   \\else\n    \\draw[very thick] (\\shift,-.29)--(\\shift,.29);\n    \\draw[very thick] (\\shift+4,-.29)--(\\shift+4,.29);\n    \\draw[->,thick,MidnightBlue] (3.65,.15)--(4.45,.15);\n    \\fill[MidnightBlue] (3.65,.15) circle (1.5pt);\n    \\fill[MidnightBlue] (4.45,.15) circle (1.5pt);\n    \\node[anchor=west,MidnightBlue] at (8.3,0) {retained};\n   \\fi\n  \\end{scope}\n }\n \\node[anchor=south] at (3.65,.43) {$y$};\n \\node[anchor=south] at (4.65,.43) {$y+h$};\n \\draw[<->] (0,1.3)--(4,1.3);\n \\node[fill=white,inner sep=2pt] at (2,1.3) {$B=4R$};\n \\draw[<->] (6,1.3)--(7,1.3);\n \\node[fill=white,inner sep=2pt] at (6.5,1.3) {$R$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A 1D diagram displaying four horizontal rows of shifted partitions:\n- Top dimension indicators:\n  - A double-headed horizontal arrow indicating length $B = 4R$ spanning 4 grid units.\n  - A double-headed horizontal arrow indicating length $R$ spanning 1 grid unit.\n  - A pair of positions $y$ and $y + h$ marked by vertical bars, connected by a blue horizontal vector with an arrow pointing from $y$ to $y + h$.\n- Four horizontal axis lines corresponding to different shift parameters:\n  - Row 1 labeled \"$\\theta = 0$\": has thin vertical tick marks spaced by $R$, and a thick vertical tick mark marking a $B$-box face that falls directly between $y$ and $y + h$; labeled on the right as \"cut\".\n  - Row 2 labeled \"$\\theta = R$\": thin ticks remain aligned with the $R$-grid, while the thick tick marks are shifted by distance $R$ to the right; the segment from $y$ to $y + h$ does not cross a thick tick; labeled on the right as \"retained\".\n  - Row 3 labeled \"$\\theta = 2R$\": thick tick marks are shifted by $2R$ to the right; labeled on the right as \"retained\".\n  - Row 4 labeled \"$\\theta = 3R$\": thick tick marks are shifted by $3R$ to the right; labeled on the right as \"retained\"."
    }
  ],
